# Release Plan — `guards-judge-every-spelling-alike`

**Milestone:** `guards-judge-every-spelling-alike` (milestone 438). The Stage-4 planning sub-task **#8605** carries the approved two-part plan, the Mode R readiness briefing it adopted (`#8605 comment 6613987950`) and the Stage-4 gate's Decision Recorded. The Stage-5 designs are on **#8628** (#8314), **#8629** (#8315) and **#8630** (#8444); their independent adversarial reviews are on **#8647**, **#8648** and **#8649**. The Collective Review scope-lock Decision Recorded is on **#8628**. The Stage-6 Engineering sub-tasks are **#8631** (#8314), **#8633** (#8444) and **#8632** (#8315); this file is Engineering Commit 0, authored by the spoke on **#8631**, which also builds #8314's slice.

**Version identity:** **versioned** — bump-class **`minor`**, provisional display **`v4.82`**. The concrete number binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim (ADR-092): the branch and this file stay slug-primary while in flight, and the Header `**Version**` cell carries the double-brace release-version placeholder, which this file reproduces in that cell only. The two sibling releases that merge first under the recorded merge order (ms#442, minor, and ms#443, major) are expected to take `v4.82` and `v5.00`, after which the claim computes **`v5.01`** for this release.

**Topology:** D-C **SINGLE** — one release branch, `release/guards-judge-every-spelling-alike`, one pull request opened in **draft** at Stage 6 Phase C2 after #8314's slice, one merge into `main`.

**Concurrency posture:** **P0 fully serial** — #8314 → #8444 → #8315, one Engineering spoke at a time, each landing on the release branch before the next starts. Force-push, `--force-with-lease` included, is not used on the shared branch.

**Release class:** `cross-cutting` — re-rendered from `novel` at the Collective Review scope-lock; Stage 9 review depth **Deep** (§ Release Class declaration).

> **Provenance and precedence.** This file transcribes the Stage-4 Release Planning output on #8605 (parts 1/2 and 2/2) and is amended by two decision records, in order: the Stage-4 gate's Decision Recorded on #8605 (plan approved; `novel` pending the Stage-5 tripwire re-check; SINGLE; P0; D-Crisp's six card-body amendments applied; D-HookDeploy (B); D-Scope-023 (A); minor), and the Collective Review scope-lock Decision Recorded on #8628 (D-S5-8314-EXITS (A), the masked exit view; #8315's bounded Stage-5 delta; the security rating; `cross-cutting`; every other design decision approved as recommended). The three Stage-5 designs and the approved findings of their adversarial reviews are design inputs as that record adopts them. Where the plan and a later record differ, the later record wins, and each such change is recorded in § Deviation Log with the decision that caused it. Every thread comment consumed is authored by the repository owner (Comment-Ingestion Trust Boundary).

## Commit-0 Version Re-Verify Record

Run at Engineering Commit 0, both halves, per `release/references/how-to/hub-spoke-bridge.md` Procedure 0 § Canonical location (single-branch topology, steps 1 to 3b).

### Version half (steps 1–3, pre-write)

| Step | Action | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | `git fetch --tags origin`, then `git fetch origin main` | both exit 0; `origin/main` = `f8d88626` (`f8d8862668aa373a37f36f309c54eb364fae2d9b`), equal to the Stage-4 baseline pin. The release branch was cut from it before this file was written |
| **2** | Recompute next-free for bump-class **`minor`** through the adapter itself: `release/tools/claim-version.sh --sha f8d8862668aa373a37f36f309c54eb364fae2d9b --bump minor --dry-run` (the adapter's own allocation; no tag pushed) | **`v4.82`** (exit 0; "would claim v4.82 (no tag pushed)") — equal to the planned display |
| **3** | HALT unless the planned version is absent from the claimed set AND equals the recomputed next-free. The tag arm binds; the published-Release and ledger arms corroborate | **absent on every arm, equal to next-free → PROCEED** |

**Probe record for the step-3 zero** (per `core/disciplines/review-discipline-principles.md` § 8, elements PV-0 to PV-7):

```
Probe:       git ls-remote --tags origin                                   (tag arm — binds)
             gh release list --limit 5000                                  (Release arm)
             git show origin/main:release/releases/RELEASE_LOG.md          (ledger arm)
             each read by one python3 line scan that compares exact version tuples
Denominator: 452 ls-remote lines (227 distinct versions, the highest v4.81); 225 published
             Releases (under the 5000 limit, so not truncated); the ledger at origin/main,
             2,892 lines
Control - sensitivity: the same scan on the v4.81 slot — 2 tag lines, 1 Release line and
             7 ledger lines. Every arm returns non-zero, so a zero on the v4.82 slot is a
             real negative
Control - specificity: NOT TRIGGERED — a slot-occupancy question over one exact version tuple
             has no near-miss class the probe must reject (the comparator is tuple equality)
Extraction:  every line of the three reads, the ledger read from origin/main (never the
             worktree copy)
Result:      0 occupants of the (4,82) slot on every arm
Verdict:     CLEAN — v4.82 is free and equals the recomputed next-free; no HALT
```

**In-flight state at Commit 0:** two sibling releases, both open in draft and neither merged — ms#442 (#8645, minor, recomputed next-free `v4.82`, the same slot) and ms#443 (#8646, major, recomputed next-free `v5.00`). Both merge ahead of this release under the recorded merge order, so a later re-derivation reads `v5.01` (§ Cross-PR Overlap Audit → In-Flight Release Roster). This is the expected state the Stage-4 plan named (R-9), and it is not a HALT at this rung: the slot binds at the claim.

### Manifest half (step 3b, post-write and pre-commit)

`release/tools/claim-version.sh --verify-stamp guards-judge-every-spelling-alike`, run after this file was written and before it was committed; exit 0 is required. The result is recorded in § Verification Evidence.

This file carries **exactly one** literal double-brace release-version placeholder — the Header `**Version**` cell. Every other mention names the placeholder rather than reproducing it, because the claim resolves the token by substitution across the whole file.

### Date variable

`${AUDIT_DATE_UTC}` is resolved **once**, at Engineering Commit 0, as `date -u +%Y-%m-%d` → **`2026-11-04`** (Wednesday). Every later card that writes a date-bearing identifier consumes this value and writes no other literal. Its one load-bearing use known at this commit is the `date:` frontmatter of #8314's decision record.

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate (operator, 2026-11-04)

Recorded on #8635 and #8634 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the DT↔Engineering pass 2 finding on #8635: every global substitution of the whole command in the BLOCK-EGRESS-007 scanner and in the deletion-guard hook's -022 segment split grows faster than the command does on the default macOS shell, `main`'s code and this release's additions alike.

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-TIMEOUT | **(A) All sites now.** A DT↔Engineering pass 3 makes every whole-command substitution in the -007 scanner (#8315) and in the -022 segment split (#8314) a linear pass, `main`'s sites and this release's alike; each hook gets a timed arm at three sizes in both locales; the Pass-2 targeted re-review then covers it |
| D-S7-RATING | **Security-relevant bug**, handled publicly in general terms: the size at which a hook would reach the runtime's timeout is extrapolated, not measured end to end |

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate, pass 4 (operator, 2026-11-04)

Recorded on #8635 and #8634 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the DT↔Engineering pass 3 finding DEV-48: a removal repeated once per step of a loop over a long text still cost three places more than linear time.

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-PASS4 | **(A) All three.** A DT↔Engineering pass 4 makes linear, under pass 3's rules (byte-identical output, timed arms in both locales, zero verdict movement): the egress continuation join, added by this release; the egress quote walk, `main`'s; and the fs-boundary refusal-message renderer, `main`'s. The hub's brief places the -022 rule's handling of a long line dense with quote characters with the quote walk |
| D-S7-REDACT | **Reword the size figures.** This file and the pull-request body restated pass 2's timings with sizes; they are reworded in general terms, per the security-relevant-bug rating, because `main` keeps the slow code until this release merges |

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate, pass 5 (operator, 2026-11-05)

Recorded on #8635 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the DT↔Engineering pass 4 finding DEV-54: further places where a hook's cost grows faster than the command, which pass 4 timed and held.

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-PASS5 | **(A) Pass 5 now.** Two of the held places are code this release adds to the egress hook — the ANSI-C span decode and the keep rule's word normalization — and on one long span or word each costs more than `main` does. Held to the standard the continuation join was held to, that this release ships no slowdown against `main`, a DT↔Engineering pass 5 makes both linear under pass 3's rules: byte-identical output, timed arms in both locales, zero verdict movement |
| D-S7-ROUTE (pass 5) | **Route `main`'s held places to their owners.** They are `main`'s code, outside this release's cards, and go to the -022 work-ceiling card (#8327) and to the fs-boundary hook's later milestone, by notice to that hub. This release's scope is unchanged. The decision shares its name with the Stage 7 Phase E decision above; the gate tells them apart |
| D-S7-ONFAILURE | **A new card, filed through intake.** The runtime can be told to block the tool call when a guard hook fails or times out, through a hook setting a newer runtime than this workspace runs provides; that closes the timeout class for every guard hook at once. It changes the settings template, so it gets its own card rather than joining this release |
| D-S7-COMMENTS | **The earlier Stage 7 comments stay as they are.** Their figures describe the fast-path strip, which never shipped and is fixed, and deleting them would break the comments that cite them |

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate, pass 6 (operator, 2026-11-05)

Recorded on #8634 and #8635 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the DT↔Engineering pass 5 output on #8635: two more places in this release's own code cost more than `main` as the command grows — the egress brace-group handling and the shared command-position canonicalizer the four hooks run — and the required "Hook test harness (macOS)" job no longer finished inside its time limit.

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-PASS6 | **(A) Sweep, then fix.** A DT↔Engineering pass 6 lists every function and top-level block this release adds or changes in the hook sources and the shared canonicalizer, from the diff against `main`; times each against `main`; returns the whole table; then fixes every row that breaks the rule below. If more than six rows qualify, it stops after timing and the hub splits the work |
| D-S7-RULE | **The no-slowdown rule is about growth.** A fixed per-call cost, such as extra process starts, is not a slowdown. A cost that grows faster than `main`'s, and is above `main`'s by the largest size the timed arms use, is |
| D-S7-CI | **Raise the hook-test job's time limit.** The timed arms stay in the required job, so a return to faster-than-linear growth still blocks a merge |

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate, the pass-6 split (operator, 2026-11-05)

Recorded on #8634 and #8635 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the pass-6 output on #8635: the pass stopped at its escape hatch, with more rows of the swept population breaking the rule than one pass may fix (DEV-65).

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-READING | **All eight rows are fixed in this release.** "Grows faster than `main`" is read over the timed range: a row whose cost ratio to `main` rises from the smallest timed size to the largest breaks the rule, even if the ratio levels off between. A row breaks when, in either locale, it costs more than `main` at the largest timed size by more than 10% and more than 50 ms, and its cost ratio to `main` there is more than 1.25 times its ratio at the smallest. Medians of repeated runs are used |
| D-S7-FACTOR | **The constant-multiple class is accepted as a residual.** A row that costs more than `main` at every timed size but whose ratio to `main` holds level or falls is not fixed in this release (DEV-70) |
| D-S7-ET2 | **The egress fast-path finding goes into the egress pass.** The widened fast path sends upper-case and escaped long command words into `main`'s own command-word walk, which grows faster than the command on those words; the egress pass confirms the cause, then makes that walk linear (DEV-69) |

**Sequencing (the hub's, from the dependencies).** The DT↔Engineering pass 7 takes #8314's rows — the shared canonicalizer's basename step, option-cluster walk and split-string scan, and the deletion hook's second canonicalizer run for its exit view — with `main` merged first and the hook-test job's limit raised in its first commit (DEV-72). The DT↔Engineering pass 8 takes #8315's rows and the fast-path finding, on pass 7's canonicalizer.

### Stage 7 inter-stage gate, pass 9 (operator, 2026-11-05)

Recorded on #8635 (Gate Register row 6). Its input was the DT↔Engineering pass 8 output on #8635, which the hub verified first: pass 8's seven fixed rows hold the rule in both locales, and its DEV-88 found three more places, all this release's cost against `main`, that break it — the look-ahead's basename removal on a long upper-case command word behind a prefix word, `main`'s line newly reached through this release's widened fast path; the unparseable branch's second reader's basename removal on a long upper-case or escaped command word, this release's code; and the brace step's expansion of a group whose words share a long prefix, this release's code, since `main` expands no brace group.

| Decision | Rendered |
|---|---|
| D-S7-P9-BASE | **Fix both basename removals in a pass 9.** Each takes the remedy pass 8 used for the walk's verb test: a call to `egress_base_gh` in place of the removal, with a same-output arm and a ratio arm each (DEV-89) |
| D-S7-BRACE | **Bound the brace expansion, and read a group past the bound as written.** The brace step computes the total size of the expansion it would build — the sum of the lengths of the words, each carrying the prefix and the suffix — from its split, before any word is built; past the bound it returns as it does for a group it does not resolve, so the word stays one token, exactly as `main` reads every brace group, and the rest of the command is still evaluated. A write spelled only inside a group past the bound is not resolved, as on `main`; the egress fragment declares it among the spellings the rule does not resolve. The bound is 65536 characters, as the shell counts them (DEV-90). **Rejected:** refusing a command whose group is past the bound — it would refuse benign commands `main` admits, a new deny with no write behind it, and a refusal keyed to a size is a verdict about cost rather than about what the command does; accepting the growth as a residual — the expansion grows as the product of two parts of the command, so a large enough group holds the hook past the runtime's command-hook timeout, after which a PreToolUse hook does not block, and the code is this release's and unmerged, so no residual is owed to anything already shipped |

### #8314 — change specification (the first card; Stage-5 design on #8628, the review's adopted findings on #8647)

**Mechanism (D-S5-8314, approved):** **WIDEN-CANON** — the shared canonicalizer resolves command position past a wrapper's own grammar, and the set stays a bounded enumeration.

- **Rows (D-S5-8314-ROWS):** **COPY** — each wrapper's option grammar is a field-for-field copy of the -022 rows (`script_wrapper_row()` in `core/hooks/block-destructive.sh`), asserted equal by new `WRAPROWS-PARITY-*` arms, with mutants shown live. The -022 walk's code is not edited.
- **Words (D-S5-8314-WORDS):** parity with -022 — `nice`, `stdbuf`, `timeout`, `if`, `while`, `until` join the set (11 wrappers, 9 keywords); the six `canon|…|missing` rows of `WRAP_DECL` are retired in the same commit, and `WRAPVOCAB-PARITY-M2` is re-pointed.
- **Match form (D-S5-8314-MATCH):** the basename of every prefix word.
- **Spelling (D-S5-8314-SPELL (A)):** one leading backslash and full-token quotes on a prefix word are normalized before lookup.
- **Precision (D-S5-8314-PREC):** the word-boundary git rules lose a match only where a wrapper's row consumes `git` as an option value or positional (a command that never runs git); declared as a precision improvement and pinned beside a refused control.
- **Exits (D-S5-8314-EXITS (A), from the review's CDF-1 (ii′a)):** one additive canonicalizer output mode, a quote-masked view the widening does not reach, read only by `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh`'s Step 1 `git` exemption and Step 2 no-target exit, so they keep today's verdicts with no new refuse→allow. #8122's per-segment scope is not implemented here.
- **Cap (FMF-1):** the ten-token prefix cap is kept and declared as a readiness residual with one corpus row, in the boundary form #8444's registry row uses.
- **Canary (FMF-2):** a second probe beside each consumer's one-line canary, whose output differs between the pre-change and post-change canonicalizer, so a version-skewed or corrupt row table fails closed; pinned as a suite arm per consumer.
- **Comments (FMF-4):** `core/hooks/block-destructive.sh` lines 144–148 (the PREC class falsifies "never remove one") and lines 3188–3190 (the canonicalizer's match form).
- **Precision figure (PRF-2):** stated as measured by this release's false-positive control, not inherited from ADR-227.

**Files:** `core/hooks/lib/command-position.awk`; the four consumers (`block-destructive.sh` comments and canary, `block-egress.sh` canary, `block-fs-boundary.sh` canary, `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh` canary and the two exits); the suites `block-destructive.test.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.test.sh`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.test.sh` and `command-position-fp-control.test.sh`; the corpus `core/hooks/tests/fixtures/walk-payload-corpus.txt` (its seed block, and `EG160`–`EG162` moved to refused); the fragments `_cross-cutting.md` (lines 46, 49 and 222 and the canonical-form table), `block-rm-prefer-trash.md`, `block-fs-boundary.md` (its G2 boundary and References entry) and `block-destructive.md` (line 23); the regenerated registry; the decision record and `core/ADRs/README.md`. **Complexity:** High — a shared primitive four always-on hooks read. **Commit shape:** the red arms run against the pre-change code in the worktree's hook CI layout first (G-PR4 evidence); the RED fixtures and the fix are pushed together; then the readiness text with the regenerated registry and the decision record.

**Follow-up pass (D-S6-APPEND (A), DEV-25), after all three cards were built.** The default walk recognizes the append form of a prefix assignment, `NAME+=value`, as an assignment word, with the skip, the prefix-cap count and the (e) mark a `NAME=value` word gets. The subscript and array spellings and every other indirect form stay #8652's. The exit view keeps its walk (DEV-26). The second probe of each consumer's version-skew canary puts an append-form word ahead of its wrapper, so a primitive without the form fails the hook closed. Arms in the five suites, red before the change and green after; corpus seed 11; the readiness text names the form, and the -020 and -023 registry rows route their remaining spellings to #8652 through the fragment's References block; decision 4 of the decision record names both assignment forms. **Files:** the canonicalizer, the four consumers' canary probe, the five suites, the corpus, `_cross-cutting.md`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.md`, `block-destructive.md`, the regenerated registry, the decision record and this file, each already a row of the matrix. **Commit shape:** the arms, the corpus rows, the fix and the canary in one commit, pushed together; then the readiness text with the regenerated registry, the decision record and this file.

**Walk-fix pass (D-S6-WALKS (A), DEV-29), shared with #8315.** BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-022's interpreter prefix walk steps over the scalar append form of a prefix assignment, `NAME+=value`, with the skip and the bookkeeping a `NAME=value` word gets (the `M-WRAP-APPEND` line), so an interpreter or `source` invocation behind it is adjudicated as its `NAME=value` twin is. An append whose value opens an array still ends the run (the `M-WRAP-APPEND-ARRAY` line), as a subscript and every other indirect form do; those stay #8652's. The predicate is spelled as BLOCK-EGRESS-007's, which #8315's half of the pass asserts. The head resolver the quoted-fragment suppression reads, `script_resolve_head()`, is unchanged: it fails closed, so the append form costs a false positive there and never an admission, and the fragment's residual list names it as a precision limit. One precision class is declared (DEV-30). Arms in the destructive suite (`BLOCK-022 append*`), red before and green after; corpus seed 12's `SW` rows; the readiness text (line 23, scope item 2 and a new item 11); the regenerated registry; and ADR-231's `## Consequences` heading, split so the ADR durability lint reads the section, nothing else in the record changed. **Files:** `block-destructive.sh`, `block-destructive.test.sh`, the corpus, `block-destructive.md`, the registry, ADR-231 and this file, each already a matrix row. **Commit shape:** the fix with its arms and corpus rows in one commit; then the readiness text with the regenerated registry, the decision record's heading and this file.

**DT↔Engineering pass 2 (D-S7-FIX (A), DEV-34).** The canonicalizer's default walk tests a token as an assignment word and as a redirection before it looks the token up as a prefix word, the order the -022 walk already uses, so an assignment whose value is a path and a redirection whose attached target is one are never read as the wrapper their last path segment names. Before the fix the walk took such a token for a wrapper that takes a positional, whose operand consumed the guarded verb behind it, in all four consumers (#8634 F-01), and kept transformation (e) from marking a PATH or anti-injection assignment behind it, or one whose own value ends in a wrapper's name (the two cross-card cases of #8444's review). The exit view's walk is unchanged. Must-refuse arms (`EXT-ORDER-*`) in the four consuming suites beside plain-form controls, the -020 and -023 cases in the destructive suite with canonical-form pins, and corpus seed 13 (DEV-38); red before the fix and green after; the both-direction differential from Engineering Commit 0. The text findings of #8634 ride in their own commits: the hook comments (F-03), the suites' skew-canary comments (F-06), the decision record's override marker (F-07), and this file's rows (F-04, F-05). **Files:** the canonicalizer, the four consuming suites, the corpus, `block-destructive.sh` (comments), the decision record and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 3 (D-S7-TIMEOUT (A), DEV-44).** The -022 separator split, `main`'s three global substitutions over the whole command, which run on every command that reaches the rule, is split first by one `tr` pass in the C locale once the command reaches `SCRIPT_LINEAR_MIN` characters, on the line marked `# linear: segments`; the three substitutions then find nothing left to replace. A shorter command keeps them alone, and a failed pass leaves the text to them, so the segments are the same on every path (DEV-46). Arms in the destructive suite: `AC-022-X-EQ` (the pass byte for byte against the substitutions, beside a near miss), `AC-022-X1` to `-X24` (the hook under a watchdog on four benign shapes at 10, 20 and 40 KB in both locales), `AC-022-X-CTL` (a copy whose pass never runs, stopped at the budget) and `AC-022-X-FB` (a copy whose `tr` is missing reads the same verdicts); red before the fix where the pre-fix hook runs past the budget. The readiness text states what the arms pin and what they do not. **Files:** `block-destructive.sh` (the split, its bound and the rule's header note), `block-destructive.test.sh`, `block-destructive.md`, the registry and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 4 (D-S7-PASS4 (A), DEV-49).** The -022 rule's quote scan, `script_qadvance`, which carries the quote state from segment to segment and is `main`'s code, scans a segment of at least `SCRIPT_LINEAR_MIN` characters by one pass of `awk` in the C locale that takes the loop's own steps, so the carried state, taint, work count and bail are the loop's (`script_qadvance_linear`, on the line marked `# linear: qadvance`; DEV-52); a shorter segment, or one whose pass fails, keeps the loop. The fs-boundary hook's refusal-message renderer, `render_token`, `main`'s substitution, renders a token of at least `FSB_LINEAR_MIN` characters by one `sed` pass in the C locale first (`# linear: render`), the substitution still running after it and finding nothing left. Arms: `AC-022-Y-EQ` (the pass against the loop over segment sequences carried from a starting state, work counts near the cap among them, beside a near miss), `AC-022-Y1` to `-Y6` (a long comment line dense with quote characters at 10, 20 and 40 KB in both locales), `AC-022-Y-CTL` and `AC-022-Y-FB`; `AC-FSB-X-EQ`, `AC-FSB-X1` to `-X6` (a refused read of a large quoted operand carrying structure throughout), `AC-FSB-X-CTL` and `AC-FSB-X-FB`; red before the fix where the pre-fix hook runs past the budget. **Files:** `block-destructive.sh`, `block-destructive.test.sh`, `block-destructive.md`, `block-fs-boundary.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.test.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.md`, the registry and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 7 (the pass-6 split; D-S7-READING, DEV-72).** Four rows of pass 6's sweep are #8314's, and each is fixed. In the shared canonicalizer, three sites of the wrapper walk this release added read their token more than once: the basename step of a prefix word (`cp_lookup`) ran a pattern removal that retries its match at every position of a word holding no slash, and the option-cluster walk (`cp_wrapper_walk`) and the split-string scan (`cp_split_scan`) copied what was left of their token at every character. The basename step now skips the removal on a word with no slash, which the removal could only leave unchanged, and the two loops step through their token by index — pass 6's design, applied unchanged. In the deletion hook, the two views — the default output and the EXIT VIEW its two allow-on-match exits read — came from two full runs of the canonicalizer on every command. The canonicalizer gains a both view (`-v CMDPOS_VIEW=both`) that emits the exit view and then the default output, each ended by a NUL byte and each as a command substitution would capture it, from one quote mask, building the default output again only when its walk's edits differ from the exit walk's; the hook reads the two with two reads from one run, and its third canary probe reads the both view (DEV-74). The default and exit views are byte for byte what they were, so the other three hooks read the same text. Arms: `AC-CP-L-EQ`, `AC-CP-L1` to `-L32` and `AC-CP-L-CTL` in the false-positive control (the three sites against the code before them, and the canonicalizer alone against a neutral twin of each long shape); `AC-CPH-D`, `-E`, `-F` and `-T` in the four consuming suites (each hook on the long shapes inside the budget); `AC-CPV-ONE`, `-EQ`, `-T1` to `-T16`, `-R-U`, `-R-C` and `-CTL` in the deletion hook's suite (one run, the same verdicts, the hook inside the budget and against one run of the canonicalizer); `CMDPOS-SKEW-02` repointed and `CMDPOS-SKEW-BOTH` added; `CMDPOS-SINGLE-SOURCE-02` reads the hook's both-view read, and `-M4` shows it. **Files:** the canonicalizer, `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh`, the four consuming suites, the false-positive control, `_cross-cutting.md`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.md`, the registry, the decision record and this file, each already a matrix row, and `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml` for the hook-test job's limit (D-S7-CI, DEV-78).

Every other whole-command substitution of the five hook sources is timed at 20 KB and reported (DEV-39). The text findings: the fragment and the decision record declare the quoted backslash or brace over-read and the corpus pins it (F-04, DEV-41); the decision record names the walk-fix pass and the counter hold (F-06); DEV-31 cites the record by its slug token and DEV-23 is routed (F-07, D-S7-ROUTE (A)). **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, the corpus, `block-egress.md`, the registry, the egress decision record and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 3 (D-S7-TIMEOUT (A), DEV-44).** Every global substitution of BLOCK-EGRESS-007's scanner is a linear pass on a piece of at least `EGRESS_LINEAR_MIN` characters — one pass of `tr`, `sed` or `awk` in the C locale, on a line marked `# linear: <site>` — `main`'s and this release's alike: the quote neutralization of a comment and of a quoted span (with this release's quoted-operator stand-ins in the same pass), the token mark strip, the stand-in restore, the redirection-operator protection, the unparseable reader's view split and the segmentation. Where every pattern is one character the substitutions still run after the pass and find nothing left; the redirection operators, two characters each, skip them. A shorter piece keeps the substitutions, and a failed pass leaves the piece to them (DEV-46). The continuation join keeps a mark-free twin of each line, read once, instead of removing marks from the line it grows, and the keep rule reads that twin (DEV-47). Arms: `AC-E007-X-EQ-<site>` for the eight marked passes, `AC-E007-X1` to `-X30` (the whole hook under a watchdog on five benign shapes at 10, 20 and 40 KB in both locales), `AC-E007-X-CTL` and `AC-E007-X-FB`; red before the fix where the pre-fix hook runs past the budget. The quote walk's per-span removals and the join's per-line tests are a removal class this pass found and holds for its own decision (DEV-48). **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-egress.md`, the registry and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 4 (D-S7-PASS4 (A), DEV-49).** The removal class pass 3 held (DEV-48) is taken out of BLOCK-EGRESS-007's scanner. A long text takes the quote walk over pieces that one `awk` pass splits at its quote characters (`egress_neutralize_long`, on the line marked `# linear: walk`): the walk steps from piece to piece instead of through the text left to scan, gathering a head, a span's body or a comment's text as parts joined once. The continuation join reads a line at a time beside its mark-free twin and tests only the line it has just added (`egress_join_state`): the grown line's trailing-backslash parity is the added line's, and only the added line's `#`s are new, the first one's text reaching back through the open text before it, which is gathered as parts and joined once. The comment test and the trailing-backslash test, `main`'s helpers that both loops call, read a long text once (`# linear: comment-open`, `# linear: trail`; DEV-51). A shorter text keeps the code before this pass, and a walk whose pass fails reads the text that way. Arms: `AC-E007-Y-EQ` (the walk, the join in both readings and the two tests, with the bound at 0 against 10^9, on a dense set of texts in both locales, beside three near misses), `AC-E007-Y1` to `-Y12` (a continuation-dense command and a command of many short quoted spans at 10, 20 and 40 KB in both locales) and `AC-E007-Y-CTL` (one sensitivity copy per loop); `AC-E007-X-FB` now repoints the walk's `awk` too, and `AC-E007-SP0-NC` reads its copy's two edited lines. **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-egress.md`, the registry and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 5 (D-S7-PASS5 (A), DEV-55).** The two held places of DEV-54 that are this release's code are made linear. The ANSI-C decode (`egress_ansic_decode`, which `egress_ansic_span` calls) decodes a body of at least `EGRESS_LINEAR_MIN` characters by `egress_ansic_decode_long`, on the line marked `# linear: ansic`: the body is split once at its backslashes (`egress_split_bs`, one word split with the backslash as its only separator and globbing off), each field is decoded as the loop decodes the text it reaches there, and the parts are joined once. The escape step of the command-word normalization (`egress_unescape_rv`) reads a long word the same way (`egress_unescape_long`, `# linear: unescape`), and on a word that ends in a lone backslash it runs the loop's own last statement once, so the bash 3.2 expansion quirk that statement carries is kept (DEV-60). The keep rule's basename test matches a long word once (`egress_base_gh`, `# linear: base`), and the case step reads a long word's basename from its last eight characters (`# linear: fold`; DEV-59). A shorter text keeps the code before this pass, and no site adds a fork. Arms: `AC-E007-Z-EQ` (the decode, the escape step over three words, the normalization with and without the case step, the basename test and the keep rule in three placements, with the bound at 0 against 10^9, on 28 dense texts in both locales, beside seven near misses), `AC-E007-Z1` to `-Z16` (a command holding one long ANSI-C span of many escapes and one whose continuation line is one long word dense with escapes, at 10, 20, 40 and 80 KB in both locales) and `AC-E007-Z-CTL` (one sensitivity copy per site); `AC-E007-Y-EQ` lifts the new functions with the walk and the join. **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-egress.md`, the registry and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 8 (the pass-6 split; D-S7-READING and D-S7-ET2, DEV-80).** The scanner's four rows of pass 6's sweep and the fast-path finding are made linear, each in its own commit with its arms. **(a)** The command-word normalization of a long brace group: the brace step splits the word once at its `{`, the text after it at its `}` and the group at its commas, each by one word split with that character as the only separator and globbing off (`egress_split_at`, on the line marked `# linear: brace`), where it took the words one at a time off the text left to split and found the braces by removals that retried at every position; the escape step takes the words from one expansion of the list (`# linear: words`); and the splice puts them into the token list as slices of one expansion, with the flags in one pass over those that are set (`# linear: splice`), where both read every element by index, which bash 3.2 finds by walking the array from its start (DEV-82). **(b)** The redirection-word test steps over a long digit run or `{name}` descriptor by its length (`# linear: descriptor`), where a prefix removal tried every shorter prefix first. **(c)** The restore of quoted operators takes the segment's tokens from one expansion of the list and gathers them into a new one (`# linear: restore`); `AC-E007-X-EQ-stand-in` reads its block's token variable (DEV-83). **(d)** The short-flag reader steps over a long run of the boolean `-i` by its length (`# linear: cluster`), where it took one off at a time. **(e)** Copies of the hook confirmed the cause first (DEV-81): the walk now reads a long command word's basename from its last eight characters (`# linear: head-word`) and tests it as the verb with `egress_base_gh` (`# linear: verb`), `main`'s two removals there; DEV-63 is settled as (e) found it. No site adds a fork, and a short text keeps the code before this pass. Arms: `AC-E007-NB-EQ`, `-NR-EQ`, `-NQ-EQ` and `-NF-EQ` (each step against the code before it, put back into a copy of the shipped hook, on a dense set of words and token lists in both locales, beside near misses), `AC-E007-NH-EQ` (the whole hook with the phase forced, against the copy with the two reads as before, beside two near misses), the ratio arms on each step's shapes at 10, 20, 40 and 80 KB in both locales against a neutral twin, inside the budget and allowed (`AC-E007-NB1` to `-NB8`, `-NR1` to `-NR16`, `-NQ1` to `-NQ8`, `-NF1` to `-NF8`, `-NH1` to `-NH16`), and one sensitivity copy per step (`-NB-CTL`, `-NR-CTL1`, `-NR-CTL2`, `-NQ-CTL`, `-NF-CTL`, `-NH-CTL`). The public-text repairs pass 7 left in the false-positive control's and the deletion hook's timed arms ride their own commit (#8314, DEV-86), and the CI limits theirs (DEV-87). **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-egress.md`, the registry, `command-position-fp-control.test.sh`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.test.sh`, `block-destructive.test.sh` (a comment), `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml` and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 9 (D-S7-P9-BASE and D-S7-BRACE, DEV-89 and DEV-90).** Three places DEV-88 found are fixed, each in its own commit with its arms. **(a)** The look-ahead behind a prefix command tests each candidate's basename with `egress_base_gh` (`# linear: look-ahead`), `main`'s plain test, and its respelled test does the same on the normalized word (`# linear: look-ahead respelled`), this release's; each was a removal that retries at every position, and copies of the hook showed the plain test carries the cost on a long upper-case word right behind a prefix word and both carry it on such a word behind a passed-over word or an option (DEV-89). **(b)** The unparseable branch's second reader tests the first word of each piece with `egress_base_gh` (`# linear: second reader`). **(c)** The brace step computes the size of the expansion it would build from its split, before it builds a word, and reads a group past the bound as written (`# bound: brace`, D-S7-BRACE, DEV-90); the egress fragment and the decision record's residuals name the group past the bound beside the nested, sequence, escape-bearing and all-empty groups. No site adds a fork, and a short word keeps the code before this pass. Arms: `AC-E007-NL-EQ` and `-NU-EQ` (each test against the code before it on the whole hook with the phase forced, on commands that reach its long-word branch densely, in both locales, beside near misses), `AC-E007-NG-EQ` (the brace step against the code before it on the dense set and on groups at and around the bound, beside three near misses at the bound), `AC-E007-NG-AT`, `-NG-PAST` and `-NG-ELSE` (a group at the bound is expanded and its write refused, a group a character past it is read as written where the copy without the bound refuses it, and a write elsewhere in the same command is refused), the ratio arms `AC-E007-NL1` to `-NL16`, `-NU1` to `-NU16` and `-NG1` to `-NG16` (the whole hook on two shapes per place at 10, 20, 40 and 80 KB in both locales against a neutral twin, inside the budget and allowed) and the sensitivity copies `-NL-CTL1`, `-NL-CTL2`, `-NU-CTL` and `-NG-CTL`. The older timed arms of the four consuming suites print the ratio of a run to its budget, not a time (DEV-86, DEV-92). **Files:** `block-egress.sh`, `block-egress.test.sh`, `block-destructive.test.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.test.sh`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.test.sh`, `block-egress.md`, the registry, the egress decision record and this file, each already a matrix row.

**DT↔Engineering pass 10 (D-S7-P10-NB, DEV-97).** The long brace group's ratio arms `AC-E007-NB1` to `-NB8` and their sensitivity control `AC-E007-NB-CTL` are held to a bound of their own, `E007_NB_BOUND`, 3.0, set from measurement on the head with the spread and the headroom stated beside it; `E007_N_BOUND`, 2.0, and every other `AC-E007-N*` block are unchanged. The ratio, timed and control helpers take an optional bound that defaults to the shared one, and only the NB block passes one. The lines keep pass 8's form: the verdict and the bound on a pass, the measured ratio and the bound on a failure, no time. No hook source, fragment or registry line changes, and no arm is added. **Files:** `block-egress.test.sh` and this file, each already a matrix row.

**A2 container determination, recorded rather than assumed.** Every card's decomposition is multi-file and structure-changing, so the Phase A2 threshold predicate selects the **GitHub sub-issue container**; the per-card Stage-6 sub-tasks the hub scaffolded (#8631, #8633, #8632) are that container, and the change units of each slice are listed in its spoke's output and as checklist rows in the pull-request body. No further sub-issues are created.

| Card | S5 | S6 | S7 | S8 | S9 | S12 | S13 | Note |

No Stage-5 skip (bias ALL; every card had an open mechanism question on a security control) and no Stage-7/8 skip (every card changes a guard's verdict). Stage 9 is release-scoped at depth **Deep**. Stages 10 and 11 are platform-satisfied (the pull-request diff; git), with the hook carrier's `--dry-run` and pre-refresh snapshot as the deployed tier's preview and rollback point. Stage 12 is release-scoped: merge, atomic claim, the release-stamped rules-mirror re-lay, the hook-tier publish per D-HookDeploy (B), hash verification. Stage 13 is release-scoped: the `G-CL10` limb owners and the 30-day outcome window; each card closes at Stage 13 when its criteria are met.

**The Stage-4 conditional rows are resolved at this commit.** WIDEN-CANON fired, so the canonicalizer and the false-positive control are unconditional. RE-HOME did not fire, yet both hooks it named are edited on another basis, and each is promoted with that basis stated: `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh` for the masked exit view (D-S5-8314-EXITS (A)) and the canary, `block-fs-boundary.sh` for the canary (the review's FMF-2). FS-ARMS and RM-ARMS fired. ADR-S5 fired: the record is named by a glob because its number binds at the Stage-12 claim (§ Deviation Log DEV-10). ROUTE-CANON fired for #8444, so the canonicalizer row names both cards. NEW-READER does not fire for #8314 or #8444 — their rows name guards whose suites already read the corpus — and stays conditional for #8315's delta. NEW-TEST-SCRIPT stays conditional for #8315's delta: #8314's design adds no suite, and the allowlist's existing globs already admit any new `*.test.sh` (its design finding 13).

**At #8315's slice** the two remaining conditions resolve. NEW-READER does not fire — the egress suite's existing corpus reader grades the new rows — so the fixtures README row is promoted on another basis, the design's Change 9 (the twin field of a respelled row). NEW-TEST-SCRIPT does not fire — the slice adds its arms to the existing egress suite — so the `*-spelling.test.sh` and script-execution allowlist rows stay unmet conditionals. The decision record's row is added as #8315's `ADD`, named by a glob for the reason DEV-10 gives.

**Non-scope reasons.** `deploy.sh`: ms#442 and ms#402 (#6195) own it, and the parity arms belong in a suite. `test-runner.sh`: the runner, edited by ms#402 (#6193). `hook-fail-closed.test.sh`: it co-locates the shipped canonicalizer by copy and needs no change for the canary. The -022 blocks of `block-destructive.sh` (ms#439, ms#440) are touched by this release only at the interpreter walk's assignment predicate (the walk-fix pass, DEV-29), at the separator split's linear pass (DT pass 3, DEV-44) and at the quote scan's linear pass (DT pass 4, DEV-49), and the egress drain writers (ms#402 #6194) are untouched regions of files this release edits. `.github/workflows/repo-integrity.yml` holds the SIGPIPE-lint wrapper list the parity table reads, and is read only.

**Zero `ADD` rows for executables** unless NEW-TEST-SCRIPT fires (then its allowlist row and CI wiring ship in the same release). **No path is renamed, moved or deleted** → the mover set is empty.

## Verification Plan

### Per-Issue Verification

Method classes: a runnable probe where one exists; a named read of a named surface; or the declared-deferred form where the grading arm belongs to a later slice. Suite-graded rows are executed by the documented runner at Stage 7 and by the PR's CI hook-test job, not by the plan verifier. **#8314's rows are bound to label-presence probes at this commit**, now that its Stage-5 design has named the arms; each of the other cards' deferred rows is bound by that card's own slice. Every red-before arm's run on the pre-change code is kept as executed evidence: G-PR4 accepts a control only once it is seen acting.

### Release-Level Verification

- [ ] **File integrity:** the delivered diff matches the unconditional rows, and every fired conditional is promoted.
- [ ] **Hook suites:** the runner reads FAIL 0, with each suite's Total at or above the pin plus the arms added. **Corpus identity:** `sha256` and row counts recorded at Stage 7 and Stage 9. At the pin: `74be7ed0…5789`, 1,313 rows (1,003 destructive with 17 `open`; 310 egress with 13 `open`).
- [ ] **Registry freshness:** Check 38 passes on the release head, and again after the rebase over ms#442.
- [ ] **CIACs:** CIAC-1 to CIAC-4.
- [ ] **Stage-12 instance verification:** after the publish, each deployed edited file under the workspace hook tier equals its merged source by SHA-256 — `lib/command-position.awk`, `block-destructive.sh`, `block-egress.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.sh`, `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh`, and the deployed script-execution allowlist under NEW-TEST-SCRIPT. The sensitivity arm is the pre-publish baseline, equal to the pre-merge sources (first 12 hex at the pin: awk `9b8fb81edcb3`, destructive `d9ce4771ae4b`, egress `172cb4074ca7`, fs-boundary `1c7cbc11b3ca`, rm-prefer-trash `7f31858dcf8a`). Then fresh-session probes: a plain-form guarded command refused, a benign command allowed. The workspace tier is the only one on this workspace.

## Delivery Strategy

| Aspect | Decision |
|---|---|
| **Implementation approach** | Sequential, dependency- and contention-ordered: posture **P0** |
| **Commit strategy** | Engineering Commit 0 (this plan only). Then per card: the red arms run against the pre-change code in the worktree's hook CI layout and kept as executed evidence (G-PR4); the RED fixtures and the fix committed and pushed together, never a failing-fixture commit pushed ahead of its fix; then the readiness text with the regenerated registry (and, for #8314, the decision record). Messages carry the `release(guards-judge-every-spelling-alike):` prefix and a reference-only `(#N)` |
| **Review approach** | One PR for the milestone (topology **SINGLE**), draft until the Stage 9 gate |
| **Rebase** | Before Stage 12, after ms#442 and ms#443 have merged: the hub brings `main` into the branch, re-runs Check 38 and the harness on the new head, and re-derives D-Version |
| **Deployment mechanism** | Git merge. Then Stage-12 B4 re-lays the release-stamped rules mirror (no mirror member changes; `bypass-mode-readiness.md` is not a member). Then the hook-tier publish per **D-HookDeploy (B)**, through the carrier `docs/scripts/setup-workspace.sh --refresh-hooks` (which `./update.sh` delegates to), sourced from the primary checkout once it is fast-forwarded to the merge. No skill package changes |
| **Stacked-base cleanup posture** | Not applicable under SINGLE |

The PR body is parser-clean: the three cards carry a close keyword only in the PR body's dedicated Issue References block; no close-family verb precedes an issue number anywhere else in the body, the title or any commit message.

---

## Rollback Strategy

| Path | When | Steps | Complexity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Per-card revert (pre-merge) | one card misbehaves on the branch | `git revert` that card's run of commits (linear history); re-run the runner. #8314's exit view and its widening land in one commit, so they revert together | Low |
| Partial revert (post-merge) | one card misbehaves after the merge | a fix branch reverting that card; republish the hook tier; verify by hash | Medium (#8314 reopens all four hooks' faces) |
| Full restore | a refusal storm or a fail-closed loop on the instance | operator: `docs/scripts/setup-workspace.sh --restore-hooks` from the pre-refresh snapshot (`--list-hook-snapshots`) for immediate relief; then revert the merge commit and republish; the version tag is never deleted | MODERATE overall |
| Forward fix | a narrow, understood defect | a fix branch through the pipeline | Low |

Ordering: never revert the deletion hook's exit-view read while the widened canonicalizer stays deployed; they ship in one commit for that reason. A partial ship of the words without the rows is safe for AC2 only with the exit view in place.

## Release Class declaration

**Class: `cross-cutting`** — re-rendered from `novel` at the Collective Review scope-lock. The named tripwire recorded in the predecessor release's plan ("if a later decision moves the wrapper vocabulary into the canonicalizer, re-render to `cross-cutting`") is met: #8314's WIDEN-CANON moves the -022 wrapper words and a copy of its option rows into the shared canonicalizer, with a four-hook blast radius. The taxonomy's formal triggers (a) and (b) do not fire; the re-render is cheaper-to-stricter. `effective_pts: 14 × 1.3 = 18`, inside the 15–25 target band. Posture: engagement density **Tight** · Stage 9 review depth **Deep** · Stage 5 activation bias **ALL** · Stage 13 outcome window **30-day**.

---

## Release Outcome Statement

Transcribed from the milestone description at Engineering Commit 0.

**AFTER** — A guarded command gets the same verdict however it is written. The shared command-position canonicalizer reaches the guarded verb behind a transparent wrapper's own options, an absolute-path wrapper, a wrapper outside its set and a compound-command condition position, so the anchored rules of the four hooks that consume it refuse what they refuse in plain form. The egress rule's own walk and the PATH-manipulation rule judge their alternate and wrapped spellings the same way.

**BEFORE** — The canonicalizer stops at a wrapper's first flag and omits several wrappers and the condition positions, so an anchored credential-read rule admits behind a wrapper what it refuses plainly, with no rollout phase to soften it. BLOCK-EGRESS-007's private walk admits six alternate spellings of a write its day-one deny refuses. BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-020 refuses a prefix-form PATH assignment and lets the same assignment through when it is handed to a wrapper.

**Actor(s):** any session whose commands pass through the guards; the deployed hook bundle.

**Success Indicator:** every member's must-refuse arms pass beside a plain-form control on a tracked differential corpus, and no corpus allow row changes verdict except a declared precision improvement; one PR for the milestone; at Stage 12 the deployed copies of every edited hook equal the merged sources.

**Limb owners:** the canonicalizer reaches the guarded verb behind a wrapper's own options, an absolute-path wrapper, a wrapper outside its set and a condition position, so the four consuming hooks' anchored rules refuse what they refuse in plain form → #8314 · the egress rule's own walk judges its alternate and wrapped spellings as it judges the plain form → #8315 · the PATH-manipulation rule judges a wrapped or otherwise respelled assignment as it judges the prefix form → #8444

The operator's decision is a strip made linear. `tr -d` in the C locale deletes the same three bytes in one pass, at a cost that grows linearly with the command and adds about 2 ms a call on a short command, and its output equals the bracket class byte for byte on a dense input with multibyte text, in both locales, beside a near miss that keeps `$` and differs (`AC-E007-T-EQ`). It reads the command from a here-string, so no argument-length limit applies, and a failure of the pass sends the command to the full scan. A regular-expression test was measured as well and rejected: in a UTF-8 locale it does not match across a byte that is not valid UTF-8, where the strip does, which would skip the scan of such a command | The operator's decision D-S7-FIX (A) (a strip made linear); the brief's own measurement instruction; Stage-6 B3 minor adjustment | **RECORDED** — Stage 9 ratification |

| **DEV-46** | **Each site keeps its substitutions below a bound, and as the fallback of a failed pass, rather than being replaced outright.** Several sites run once per quoted span, comment, token or line rather than once per command, and a pass costs one fork: replaced outright, a command of many short pieces would fork once per piece, and every short command would pay a fork at every site. So a piece of at least 256 characters (`EGRESS_LINEAR_MIN`, `SCRIPT_LINEAR_MIN`) takes the pass, and a shorter piece keeps the substitutions, whose cost the bound keeps below one fork's: a short command adds no fork, and a long one adds at most one per bound's worth of text at a looped site. Where every pattern is one character the substitutions still run after the pass and find nothing left to replace, one scan of the piece, so each such site is its shipped code with one line in front of it; the redirection operators, two characters each, skip them once their `sed` pass has made all four. A failed pass — no fork, no temporary file for the here-string, a missing tool — leaves the piece to the substitutions, the hook's own rule that every fallible step carry a fallback, since a failure inside a function would end the hook with a status that does not block; in that degraded state a long command is slow again and never judged differently. `AC-E007-X-FB` and `AC-022-X-FB` pin the fallback | The brief's "one fork at most per site group" and its per-call cost; the hooks' fallback discipline; Stage-6 B3 minor adjustment | **RECORDED** — Stage 9 ratification |

Two loops of the -007 scanner are its shapes: the quote walk, `main`'s code, which removes a prefix and a suffix of the text left to scan at each quoted span (a command of many short quoted spans), and the continuation join, this release's own, which tests the line it has grown for trailing backslashes and an open comment at each join (a command of many continuation lines — on that shape the join is this release's regression, since `main` carries no join). ## Change Description

Authored by the third and last Stage 6 Engineering spoke, once all three cards were built on this branch, and refreshed by #8314's follow-up pass (DEV-25), by the walk-fix pass on #8314 and #8315 (DEV-29), by the DT↔Engineering pass 2 (DEV-34), by the DT↔Engineering pass 3 (DEV-44), by the DT↔Engineering pass 4 (DEV-49), by the DT↔Engineering pass 5 (DEV-55), by the DT↔Engineering pass 7 (DEV-72), by the DT↔Engineering pass 8 (DEV-80), by the DT↔Engineering pass 9 (DEV-89, DEV-90), by the DT↔Engineering pass 10 (DEV-97), by the DT↔Engineering pass 11 (DEV-100 to DEV-105), by the DT↔Engineering pass 12 (DEV-106 to DEV-108) and by the DT↔Engineering pass 13 (DEV-109 to DEV-112), per `release/governance/RELEASE_PROTOCOL.md` § Change Description Protocol. Operator-facing and pre-merge; distinct from the user-facing release note authored at Stage 13 Close.

### Outcome

A guarded command now gets the same verdict however it is written. The shared command-position canonicalizer reaches the guarded verb behind a transparent wrapper's own options, an absolute-path wrapper, a wrapper that was missing from its set, an `if`/`while`/`until` condition, and a prefix assignment written in its append form as well as its plain form, so the anchored rules of the four hooks that consume it refuse what they refuse in plain form; the PATH-manipulation and anti-injection rules read an assignment at every position the canonicalizer resolves; and BLOCK-EGRESS-007 reads a gh api write the way the shell resolves it — refused from day one at the head of a segment in every spelling, and recorded at the unchanged rollout phase everywhere else. The two walks that keep their own prefix logic — the script-execution rule's interpreter walk and BLOCK-EGRESS-007's head walk — step over the append form of a prefix assignment as they step over the plain form, so an interpreter invocation behind it is adjudicated and a gh api write behind it is recorded, as each is behind the plain form. The canonicalizer reads a prefix-run assignment or redirection as what it is before it looks a word up as a wrapper, so a value or target whose last path segment names a wrapper no longer hides the command behind it, and BLOCK-EGRESS-007's fast path costs one linear pass on every command. Every whole-command substitution of BLOCK-EGRESS-007's scanner and of the -022 separator split is a linear pass on a long piece, byte for byte the substitutions it stands in front of, so neither hook's substitutions cost more than linear time in the command unless a pass itself fails, when they run as they ran before. The scanner's quote walk and continuation join, the script-execution rule's quote scan and the filesystem guard's refusal message are linear too: a long command is read once, as pieces or a line at a time, where those loops re-read the text left to scan or the line they had grown, and the guard renders a large operand by one pass. The scanner's ANSI-C decode and command-word normalization read a long span or word once as well, so one long span of many escapes, or one long word dense with escapes on a line the join would take, no longer costs more than linear time in this release's own code. The shared canonicalizer's wrapper walk reads a long command word, option cluster or split string once, as `main`'s walk reads a long word, and the deletion hook takes both of the views it reads from one run of the canonicalizer instead of two, so on the long shapes the four consuming hooks grow at `main`'s rate. The egress scanner's brace step with the splice it feeds, its redirection-word test, its restore of quoted operators and its short-flag reader read a long word, run or segment once, and the walk reads a long command word's basename once, so the upper-case and escaped spellings the widened fast path admits cost the walk what the plain spelling does; the look-ahead behind a prefix command and the unparseable branch's second reader test a long word's basename once too. The brace step bounds the expansion it builds: a group whose words share a long prefix or suffix, which would expand to the product of that text's length and the number of words, is read as written past the bound, as `main` reads every brace group, so its cost grows with the command, and a write spelled only inside such a group is declared unresolved, as on `main`. Other work measured to grow faster than what it reads is declared: `main`'s is routed to its owners (DEV-56); and the rows that cost a constant multiple above `main` are a declared residual (DEV-70, DEV-79).

### Issues resolved

| # | Outcome (one line) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| #8314 | The canonicalizer walks each wrapper's own option grammar from an asserted copy of the script walk's rows, matches a prefix word by basename, and gains `nice`/`stdbuf`/`timeout` and the condition keywords; the deletion hook's two allow-on-match exits read a frozen view and keep today's verdicts; a follow-up pass steps over the append form of a prefix assignment as the plain form is stepped over (D-S6-APPEND (A)); a walk-fix pass does the same in the script-execution rule's interpreter walk, with one precision class declared and its head resolver's plain-form test kept as a declared limit (D-S6-WALKS (A)); the DT pass 2 has the walk read a prefix-run assignment or redirection before the wrapper lookup, so a value or target that ends in a wrapper's name no longer consumes the guarded verb (D-S7-FIX (A)); the DT pass 3 splits a long command into the script-execution rule's segments by one linear pass (D-S7-TIMEOUT (A)); the DT pass 4 carries the rule's quote state across a long segment by one linear pass, and has the filesystem guard render a refusal's large operand by one (D-S7-PASS4 (A)); the DT pass 7 has the canonicalizer's basename step, option-cluster walk and split-string scan read their token once, and gives the deletion hook both of its views from one run of the canonicalizer (the pass-6 split, D-S7-READING) | DONE — its corpus seed block deferred to the Stage 9 gate (DEV-42); the constant above `main` a declared residual (DEV-79) |
| #8444 | BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-020 and -023 refuse a PATH or anti-injection assignment handed to a wrapper, behind another assignment or a redirection, after a keyword, at a condition position, inside a group, in a declaration builtin's argument list or with the append operator; the DT pass 2 declares what each rule does not judge and narrows the anti-injection guarantee to what -023 enforces | DONE — its remaining spellings owned by #8652 (DEV-16, DEV-35) |
| #8315 | BLOCK-EGRESS-007 refuses on day one a head-of-segment write reached through a leading redirection, a continuation between the verb and its subcommand, an escaped, brace-expanded, ANSI-C- or locale-quoted or case-variant verb, a quote-split verb, or an attached or clustered method flag, in a command its scanner can tokenize and in one it cannot; behind a prefix command each is recorded as its plain twin is; a walk-fix pass steps its head walk over the append form of a prefix assignment, holding both first-invocation counters so no day-one refusal moves (D-S6-WALKS (A)); the DT pass 2 makes the fast path one linear pass (D-S7-FIX (A)), the DT pass 3 every substitution of the scanner (D-S7-TIMEOUT (A)), the DT pass 4 its quote walk and continuation join (D-S7-PASS4 (A)), the DT pass 5 its ANSI-C decode and command-word normalization (D-S7-PASS5 (A)), the DT pass 8 its brace step, redirection-word test, quoted-operator restore, short-flag reader and the walk's basename reads of a long command word (the pass-6 split, D-S7-READING and D-S7-ET2), and the DT pass 9 the look-ahead's and the second reader's basename tests, with a bound on the brace step's expansion (D-S7-P9-BASE, D-S7-BRACE) | DONE — the unrecorded residual spellings, the brace group past the bound among them, owned by #8650 (DEV-23, DEV-35, DEV-94); `main`'s measured sites of the cost class routed (DEV-56); the constant above `main` a declared residual (DEV-70) |

### Key decisions

- **D-S5-8314 WIDEN-CANON**, with rows COPY, word parity, a basename match and SPELL (A): the canonicalizer, not a re-homed walk, carries the wrapper grammar, and a parity arm keeps its copy of the rows equal to the script walk's (§ Decision Record).
- **D-S5-8314-EXITS (A), the masked exit view:** the two allow-on-match exits read an un-widened view, so the widening adds no refusal-to-allow there and #8122 stays its own card.
- **D-S5-8444 ROUTE-CANON with D-Scope-023 (A):** the assignment-anchored rules ride the canonicalizer's new assignment branch, and -023's newly refused positions are a declared tightening.
- **D-S5-8315 NO-ADOPT-CANON, with the delta as decided:** the egress rule keeps its own walk; a respelling takes the rung of the position the shell resolves it to; a respelled invocation counts on its own counter; the unparseable branch reads the same spellings; the fast path is derived from its transformations; an ANSI-C span keeps the shipped terminator; and a method or field flag in its attached or clustered short form joined the card as its tenth class.
- **Security rating — security-relevant bug:** each card's RED fixtures landed in its own fix commit after a red-before run kept as executed evidence.
- **D-S6-APPEND (A)**, at the Stage 6 inter-stage gate: a follow-up pass on #8314 has the canonicalizer's default walk step over the append form of a prefix assignment, with the exit view left frozen (DEV-26), the precision class it reaches declared (DEV-27), and each consumer's version-skew canary extended to tell a primitive without the form apart.
- **D-S6-WALKS (A)**, at the same gate: a walk-fix pass on #8314 and #8315 has the script-execution rule's interpreter walk and BLOCK-EGRESS-007's head walk step over the scalar append form with the plain form's skip, their predicate spelled identically and asserted by a parity arm; the egress walk holds both first-invocation counters for an invocation behind the form (DEV-31); one precision class is declared in each walk (DEV-30, DEV-32) and a quoted-spelling movement in the egress walk (DEV-33); the script-execution rule's head resolver keeps its plain-form test as a declared precision limit.
- **D-S7-FIX (A)**, at Stage 7 Phase E: one DT↔Engineering pass for all three cards — the canonicalizer's test order, the fast path made linear (by a `tr` pass, DEV-37), `main` merged in with the ADR renumber (DEV-36), and the text findings, the anti-injection guarantee among them.
- **D-S7-ROUTE (A)**, at the same gate: the operand and indirect assignment spellings to #8652, DEV-23's four egress spellings to #8650, the nested-shell limit and the prefix cap accepted as documented design limits, and the remaining out-of-scope items to the later hook milestones' hub (DEV-35).
- **D-S7-TIMEOUT (A)**, at the Stage 7 inter-stage gate: a DT↔Engineering pass 3 makes every whole-command substitution of BLOCK-EGRESS-007's scanner and of the -022 split a linear pass, `main`'s sites and this release's alike, with timed arms in both hooks (DEV-44); each site keeps its substitutions below a bound and as its fallback (DEV-46), and the join keeps a mark-free twin of each line (DEV-47).
- **D-S7-RATING**, at the same gate: the class is a security-relevant bug, described publicly in general terms (DEV-45).
- **D-S7-PASS4 (A)**, at the same gate on pass 3's held finding: a DT↔Engineering pass 4 makes linear the egress continuation join, its quote walk with the -022 rule's quote scan, and the fs-boundary refusal renderer, under pass 3's rules (DEV-49); the two tests both loops call read a long text once (DEV-51), and the -022 scan's long path replicates its loop (DEV-52).
- **D-S7-REDACT**, at the same gate: the size figures this file and the pull-request body restated from pass 2 are reworded in general terms (DEV-50).
- **D-S7-PASS5 (A)**, at the same gate on pass 4's held finding: a DT↔Engineering pass 5 makes the egress ANSI-C decode and the keep rule's word normalization linear, this release's own code, under pass 3's rules (DEV-55); the case step's basename read (DEV-59) and the trailing-backslash test's answer for a text that does not end in a backslash (DEV-62) with them, a bash 3.2 quirk of the escape step kept (DEV-60), and the head's small constant difference from `main` at the smallest sizes recorded (DEV-63).
- **D-S7-ROUTE (pass 5), D-S7-ONFAILURE and D-S7-COMMENTS**, at the same gate: `main`'s held places to the -022 work-ceiling card and the fs-boundary hook's later milestone (DEV-56); the guard-hook failure setting to its own card (DEV-57); the earlier Stage 7 comments left as posted (DEV-58).
- **D-S7-PASS6 (A), D-S7-RULE and D-S7-CI**, at the same gate on pass 5's finding: a pass that sweeps the whole changed population against `main` and fixes every row that breaks a rule about growth, not fixed cost; it ran to its escape hatch and committed nothing (DEV-65, DEV-67); the hook-test job's limit raised, its timed arms kept in the required job (DEV-78).
- **D-S7-READING, D-S7-FACTOR and D-S7-ET2**, at the same gate on pass 6's table: the endpoint reading, with all eight rows fixed in this release across two passes (DEV-66) — #8314's four by pass 7 (DEV-72 to DEV-74) and #8315's four by pass 8, with the fast-path finding (DEV-80 to DEV-83); the constant-multiple class accepted as a residual with #8660 its systemic remedy (DEV-70, DEV-79); the egress fast-path finding to pass 8, which confirmed its cause before the fix (DEV-69, DEV-81).
- **D-S7-P9-BASE and D-S7-BRACE**, at the same gate on pass 8's finding: the look-ahead's and the second reader's basename removals replaced by `egress_base_gh` (DEV-89); the brace step's expansion bounded, a group past the bound read as written, with refusal and acceptance as a residual rejected (DEV-90).
- **D-S7-P10-NB**, at the same gate on pass 9's DEV-96: the long-brace-group ratio arms and their control held to a bound of their own, set from measurement with headroom over the head's largest reading, the shared bound and every other arm unchanged, no hook code touched (DEV-97).
- **D-S7-PE2**, at Stage 7 Phase E on the targeted re-review: the three PASS verdicts stand, and a DT↔Engineering pass 11 rewords the code comments that paired a size class or a growth rate with the runtime's timeout and sweeps the release's text for the pattern (DEV-100), declares the constant-multiple class and its follow-up card in the egress fragment and decision record (DEV-101), pins the walk's eight-character basename stand-in with one arm (DEV-102), and reconciles two lines of this file (DEV-103); no guard logic changes.
- **D-S7-ADR**, at the Stage 7 → 8 boundary: `main`'s sibling framing record took ADR-230, so a DT↔Engineering pass 12 merges `main` and the renumber tool moves this release's egress record to ADR-232 now rather than at Stage 12, with the pull request body's mentions edited by hand; no hook or test changes (DEV-106, DEV-107).
- **D-S8-PE**, at Stage 8 Phase E: #8314, #8315 and #8444 are each CONDITIONAL ACCEPT; the remaining conditions are acts at the Stage 9 GO — accepting each card's residual set with its successors, and ratifying #8314's deferred seed block (DEV-42).
- **D-S8-8314-AC2 and D-S8-8444-AC1**, at the same gate: #8314's AC-2 is amended to carry the declared-precision exception #8315's AC-2 already has, and reads MET as amended (DEV-109); #8444's AC-1 residual clause is amended to admit the shared canonicalizer's declared limits, a program string handed to another shell and an assignment behind the prefix-run cap, with a successor card for the nested-shell class held for the Stage 9 filing sweep (DEV-110); neither owes an Operator Override Record.
- **D-S8-P13**, at the same gate: a DT↔Engineering pass 13 rewords the egress timed-arm header to the class and reports, without changing them, the other sentences of the release's files that pair a command size with a run stopped by a budget or a timeout (DEV-111, DEV-112); it reconciles this file for Stage 8 and changes no code.
- **D-ReleaseClass `cross-cutting`, D-C SINGLE, P0, D-HookDeploy (B), D-Version minor** — as § Decision Record renders them.

### Reversibility

**MODERATE — HIGH confidence.** Before the merge, `git revert` of one card's run of commits withdraws that card alone, and the follow-up pass's fix and its canary probe share one commit, so they revert together; each card's half of the walk-fix pass is one fix commit and one readiness commit, and reverting #8315's half alone leaves #8314's intact; each card's DT pass-2 fix is one commit with its arms, revertible alone, and so is each card's DT pass-3 fix, whose revert restores the substitutions alone, slower and byte for byte the same, and each DT pass-4 fix — the egress walk and join, the -022 quote scan, the fs-boundary renderer — whose revert restores the loop before it, likewise, as does the DT pass-5 fix, the egress decode and normalization with its arms in one commit; the DT pass 7's two fixes — the canonicalizer's three sites, and the deletion hook's both view with the canonicalizer mode it reads — are one commit each with their arms, the second revertible before the first, and its time-limit commit stands alone; the DT pass 8's five fixes are one commit each with their arms, each revertible alone, and its public-text and time-limit commits stand alone; the DT pass 9's three fixes are one commit each with their arms, each revertible alone, and its public-text commit and time-limit commit stand alone; the DT pass 10's bound is one commit to the egress suite, revertible alone, which puts the long-brace-group arms back on the shared bound; the merge of `main` is never reverted on its own; after it, a revert of the merge commit restores `main`, and the reverted hooks reach an instance only through a republish by the hook carrier, verified by SHA-256 — the version tag is retained, never deleted.

### Downstream impact

- The Stage-12 hook publish carries five edited files: `core/hooks/lib/command-position.awk`, `block-destructive.sh`, `block-egress.sh`, `block-fs-boundary.sh` and `block-rm-prefer-trash.sh`. Each consumer's version-skew canary also tells apart a primitive without the append form of a prefix assignment, so a stale primitive beside the published hooks fails them closed: the two always-enforce hooks deny, and the two mode-capable hooks deny in enforce and degrade to the raw command in warn.
- #8652 owns the spellings BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-020 and -023 still admit — `env`'s split-string and unset-option spellings, a program's own variable operand, and the indirect spellings — and the two rules' registry rows route them to it.
- BLOCK-EGRESS-007's graduation card (#6195) inherits every recorded respelled write behind a prefix command, and the drain card (#6194) the populations they land in; #8650 owns the respelled spellings still admitted with no record. The walk-fix pass adds two to those populations: a write behind the append form of a prefix assignment is a head-walk row, and one behind a prefix word followed by that form moves from the look-ahead rows to the head-walk rows at the same verdict.
- #8652 still owns the array append, the subscripts and every other indirect form of an assignment, in both walks the walk-fix pass changed as in the canonicalizer.
- Sibling milestones rebase onto this release's text: ms#441 onto `block-fs-boundary.md` line 49; ms#439 and ms#440 onto `block-destructive.md` line 23 and the corpus; ms#402 onto `block-egress.sh`, its fragment and its suite. ms#442 and ms#443 have merged, and `main` is merged into this branch (DEV-34, DEV-36), again during pass 3 after their Stage 12 and 13 close-outs, and again during pass 12 after a third sibling release took ADR-230 (DEV-106).
- Routed, not built here: the case-variant command class in the other hooks' anchored rules (AI-002) and the hook carrier's `post-merge` re-point (AI-003).
- CIAC-2's card-seed-header count reads 2 of the 3 planned, because #8314's seed block is deferred; the follow-up pass's seed 11 is headed so that it does not count as that block (DEV-28), and so are the later passes' seeds 12 to 14 (DEV-38).
- The decision records are ADR-231 (the command-position canonicalizer, renumbered from 229 when `main`'s filing-threshold record took that number) and ADR-232 (the egress respelling rung, renumbered from 230 when `main`'s framing record took that number), each cited in this release by its slug token (DEV-36, DEV-106).
- Changed by pass 3: every whole-command substitution of BLOCK-EGRESS-007's scanner and of the -022 segment split is a linear pass on a long piece, `main`'s code with this release's additions (DEV-39, DEV-44). Changed by pass 4: the scanner's quote walk (`main`'s) and continuation join (this release's), the -022 rule's quote scan (`main`'s) and the fs-boundary hook's refusal renderer (`main`'s) are linear on a long command (DEV-49). Changed by pass 5: the scanner's ANSI-C decode and command-word normalization (this release's) are linear on a long span or word (DEV-55). Routed: `main`'s sites of the cost class, to the -022 work-ceiling card (#8327) and the fs-boundary hook's later milestone (DEV-56); a new card for the guard-hook failure setting (DEV-57). Changed by pass 7: the shared canonicalizer's basename step, option-cluster walk and split-string scan (this release's) read their token once, and the canonicalizer gains a both view that the deletion hook reads in place of two runs (DEV-72, DEV-74); the published canonicalizer and deletion hook move together, since the hook's canary refuses a canonicalizer without the both view. Changed by pass 8: the egress scanner's brace step and splice, redirection-word test, quoted-operator restore and short-flag reader (this release's), and the walk's two basename reads of a long command word (`main`'s, reached newly through the widened fast path) read their text once (DEV-80, DEV-81). Changed by pass 9: the look-ahead's two basename tests (`main`'s plain test and this release's respelled one) and the second reader's (this release's) test a long word once, and the brace step reads a group past its bound as written, which `main` does with every group (DEV-89, DEV-90). Accepted as a residual: the constant-multiple class (DEV-70, DEV-79), with #8660 its systemic remedy; since pass 11 the egress fragment and its decision record declare the class and name #8660 in their reference blocks (DEV-101).
- The required hook-test job's limit is 110 minutes, and the install-regression job's is 260 with its two gating steps at 120 each (DEV-78, DEV-87, DEV-93); the harness's timed arms stay in both.
